@[reducible]
def twoSum_precond (nums : List Int) (target : Int) : Prop :=
  let pairwiseSum := List.range nums.length |>.flatMap (fun i =>
    nums.drop (i + 1) |>.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y))
  nums.length > 1 ∧ pairwiseSum.count target = 1

def findComplement (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (i : Nat) (x : Int) : Option Nat :=
  let rec aux (nums : List Int) (j : Nat) : Option Nat :=
    match nums with
    | []      => none
    | y :: ys => if x + y = target then some (i + j + 1) else aux ys (j + 1)
  aux nums 0

def twoSumAux (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (i : Nat) : Prod Nat Nat :=
  match nums with
  | []      => panic! "No solution exists"
  | x :: xs =>
    match findComplement xs target i x with
    | some j => (i, j)
    | none   => twoSumAux xs target (i + 1)

def twoSum (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (h_precond : twoSum_precond (nums) (target)) : Prod Nat Nat :=
  twoSumAux nums target 0

@[reducible]
def twoSum_postcond (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (result: Prod Nat Nat) (h_precond : twoSum_precond (nums) (target)) : Prop :=
  let i := result.fst;
  let j := result.snd;
  (i < j) ∧
  (i < nums.length) ∧ (j < nums.length) ∧
  (nums[i]!) + (nums[j]!) = target

lemma findComplement_some {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} {j : Nat} :
  findComplement xs target i x = some j → i < j ∧ j <= i + xs.length ∧ ∃ k, k < xs.length ∧ j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
  -- By definition of `findComplement`, if it returns `some j`, then `j` is the index of the complement of `x` in `xs`.
  intro h_findComplement
  obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ : ∃ k < xs.length, j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
    -- By definition of `findComplement`, if it returns `some j`, then there exists some `k` in `xs` where `x + k = target` and `j` is the index of that `k` in `xs`. This follows directly from the definition of `findComplement`.
    have h_findComplement_spec : ∀ {xs : List ℤ} {target : ℤ} {i : ℕ} {x : ℤ} {j : ℕ}, findComplement xs target i x = some j → List.length xs ≥ 1 ∧ ∃ k < List.length xs, j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
      intros xs target i x j h_findComplement; induction' xs with xs_hd xs_tl ih generalizing i x j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement ] ;
      · cases h_findComplement;
      · by_cases h : x + xs_hd = target <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ];
        obtain ⟨ k, hk₁, hk₂, hk₃ ⟩ := ih ( show findComplement.aux target ( i + 1 ) x xs_tl 0 = Option.some j from by
                                              convert h_findComplement using 1;
                                              -- By definition of `findComplement.aux`, we can prove this by induction on the list `xs_tl`.
                                              have h_aux_eq : ∀ {xs : List ℤ} {target : ℤ} {i : ℕ} {x : ℤ} {j : ℕ}, findComplement.aux target (i + 1) x xs j = findComplement.aux target i x xs (j + 1) := by
                                                intros xs target i x j; induction' xs with xs_hd xs_tl ih generalizing i x j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ] ;
                                                ring;
                                              exact h_aux_eq );
        exact ⟨ hk₁ + 1, by linarith, by linarith, by simpa [ List.getElem?_cons ] using hk₃.2 ⟩;
    exact h_findComplement_spec h_findComplement |>.2;
  exact ⟨ by linarith, by linarith, k, hk.1, hk.2.1, hk.2.2 ⟩

lemma findComplement_none {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} :
  findComplement xs target i x = none → ∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target := by
  intro h k hk_lt hk_eq
  have h_aux_none : ∀ (xs : List Int) (j : Nat), findComplement.aux target i x xs j = none → ∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target := by
    -- By induction on the list xs, we can show that if findComplement.aux returns none, then there's no index k in xs where x + xs[k]! equals the target.
    intros xs j hj_none k hk_lt hk_eq
    induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing j k;
    · contradiction;
    · rcases k with ( _ | k ) <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ];
      grind;
  -- Apply the lemma h_aux_none to the hypothesis h.
  apply h_aux_none xs 0 h k hk_lt hk_eq

lemma findComplement_eq_none {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} :
  (∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target) → findComplement xs target i x = none := by
  -- By definition of `findComplement`, if no element in `xs` satisfies `x + y = target`, then `findComplement xs target i x` returns `none`.
  intros h_no_complement
  induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing i x <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement ];
  · -- In the base case, when the list is empty, the function returns none.
    simp [findComplement.aux];
  · -- By definition of `findComplement.aux`, if no element in `y :: ys` satisfies `x + y = target`, then `findComplement.aux target i x (y :: ys) 0` returns `none`.
    have h_aux_none : ∀ {j : ℕ} {xs : List ℤ}, (∀ k < xs.length, x + xs[k]! ≠ target) → findComplement.aux target i x xs j = none := by
      intros j xs h_no_complement; induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ] ;
      -- Apply the induction hypothesis to the list `ys` and the parameter `j + 1`.
      have h_ind : findComplement.aux target i x ys (j + 1) = none := by
        exact ih fun k hk => h_no_complement ( k + 1 ) ( by linarith );
      exact if_neg ( h_no_complement 0 ( Nat.zero_lt_succ _ ) ) |> fun h => h.trans h_ind;
    grind

              · grind;
              · have := twoSumAux_prop xs target ( 1 + i ) ; simp_all +decide [ add_comm 1 ] ;
          · exact Eq.symm ( by rw [ show twoSumAux ( x :: xs ) target i = twoSumAux xs target ( i + 1 ) from by { rw [ show twoSumAux ( x :: xs ) target i = match findComplement xs target i x with | some j => ( i, j ) | none => twoSumAux xs target ( i + 1 ) from rfl ] ; aesop } ] );
        · convert hv_lt.2 using 1 ; ring;
          simp +arith +decide [ add_assoc, add_tsub_add_eq_tsub_left ];
    · obtain ⟨ j, hj ⟩ := Option.ne_none_iff_exists'.mp h;
      -- By definition of `twoSumAux`, if `findComplement xs target i x = some j`, then `twoSumAux (x :: xs) target i = (i, j)`.
      have h_twoSumAux : twoSumAux (x :: xs) target i = (i, j) := by
        exact Eq.symm ( by rw [ show twoSumAux ( x :: xs ) target i = ( i, j ) from by { rw [ twoSumAux ] ; aesop } ] );
      -- Since $findComplement xs target i x = some j$, we have $i < j$ and $j \leq i + xs.length$.
      have h_j_bounds : i < j ∧ j ≤ i + xs.length := by
        exact findComplement_some hj |> fun ⟨ hj₁, hj₂, hj₃ ⟩ => ⟨ hj₁, hj₂ ⟩;
      -- By definition of `findComplement`, if `findComplement xs target i x = some j`, then `x + (xs.get! (j - (i + 1))) = target`.
      have h_findComplement : x + (xs.get! (j - (i + 1))) = target := by
        have := findComplement_some hj; aesop;
      simp_all +decide [ Nat.sub_sub ];
      exact ⟨ by linarith, by rw [ show j - i = j - ( i + 1 ) + 1 by omega ] ; aesop ⟩

lemma mem_pairwiseSum_iff_exists_pair {nums : List Int} {x : Int} :
  x ∈ (List.range nums.length |>.flatMap (fun i => nums.drop (i + 1) |>.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y))) ↔
  ∃ i j, i < j ∧ j < nums.length ∧ nums[i]! + nums[j]! = x := by
  constructor;
  · simp +zetaDelta at *;
    intro i hi hx;
    rw [ List.mem_iff_get ] at hx;
    obtain ⟨ n, hn ⟩ := hx;
    use i, n + i + 1;
    grind;
  · -- If there exist indices i and j such that i < j, j < nums.length, and nums[i]! + nums[j]! = x, then x is in the flatMap of the pairwise sums.
    intro h
    obtain ⟨i, j, hij, hj, hx⟩ := h
    have h_exists : x ∈ List.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y) (List.drop (i + 1) nums) := by
      rw [ List.mem_iff_get ];
      use ⟨ j - ( i + 1 ), by
        simp +arith +decide [ hj.le ];
        omega ⟩
      generalize_proofs at *;
      grind;
    exact List.mem_flatMap.mpr ⟨ i, List.mem_range.mpr ( by linarith ), h_exists ⟩

lemma precond_implies_exists_pair {nums : List Int} {target : Int} :
  twoSum_precond nums target → ∃ i j, i < j ∧ j < nums.length ∧ nums[i]! + nums[j]! = target := by
  -- By definition of `twoSum_precond`, if the condition holds, then there exists a unique pair (i, j) such that i < j and nums[i]! + nums[j]! = target. This follows from the fact that the count of the target in the pairwise sums is 1.
  intros h_precond
  have h_pair : ∃ i j, i < j ∧ j < nums.length ∧ nums[i]! + nums[j]! = target := by
    have h_count : (List.range nums.length |>.flatMap (fun i => nums.drop (i + 1) |>.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y))).count target = 1 := by
      grind
    have h_pair : target ∈ (List.range nums.length |>.flatMap (fun i => nums.drop (i + 1) |>.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y))) := by
      -- Since the count of the target in the list is 1, the target must be present in the list.
      have h_target_in_list : target ∈ List.flatMap (fun i => List.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y) (List.drop (i + 1) nums)) (List.range nums.length) := by
        have h_count_pos : 0 < List.count target (List.flatMap (fun i => List.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y) (List.drop (i + 1) nums)) (List.range nums.length)) := by linarith
        contrapose! h_count_pos; simp_all +decide [ List.count_eq_zero_of_not_mem ] ;
      assumption;
    exact?;
  -- Apply the hypothesis `h_pair` to conclude the proof.
  apply h_pair

theorem twoSum_spec_satisfied (nums: List Int) (target: Int) (h_precond : twoSum_precond (nums) (target)) :
    twoSum_postcond (nums) (target) (twoSum (nums) (target) h_precond) h_precond := by
  -- By `precond_implies_exists_pair`, there exists a pair (i, j) such that i < j and nums[i]! + nums[j]! = target.
  obtain ⟨i, j, hij, h_pair⟩ : ∃ i j, i < j ∧ j < nums.length ∧ nums[i]! + nums[j]! = target := by

/-
This module implements a solution to the Two Sum problem in Lean 4.
It defines `twoSum` which takes a list of integers and a target integer, along with a proof that a unique solution exists.
It returns a pair of indices such that the sum of the numbers at those indices equals the target.
The correctness of the implementation is proven in `twoSum_spec_satisfied`.
-/

import Mathlib

set_option linter.mathlibStandardSet false

open scoped BigOperators
open scoped Real
open scoped Nat
open scoped Classical
open scoped Pointwise

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option maxRecDepth 4000
set_option synthInstance.maxHeartbeats 20000
set_option synthInstance.maxSize 128

set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

@[reducible]
def twoSum_precond (nums : List Int) (target : Int) : Prop :=
  let pairwiseSum := List.range nums.length |>.flatMap (fun i =>
    nums.drop (i + 1) |>.map (fun y => nums[i]! + y))
  nums.length > 1 ∧ pairwiseSum.count target = 1

def findComplement (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (i : Nat) (x : Int) : Option Nat :=
  let rec aux (nums : List Int) (j : Nat) : Option Nat :=
    match nums with
    | []      => none
    | y :: ys => if x + y = target then some (i + j + 1) else aux ys (j + 1)
  aux nums 0

def twoSumAux (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (i : Nat) : Prod Nat Nat :=
  match nums with
  | []      => panic! "No solution exists"
  | x :: xs =>
    match findComplement xs target i x with
    | some j => (i, j)
    | none   => twoSumAux xs target (i + 1)

def twoSum (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (h_precond : twoSum_precond (nums) (target)) : Prod Nat Nat :=
  twoSumAux nums target 0

@[reducible]
def twoSum_postcond (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (result: Prod Nat Nat) (h_precond : twoSum_precond (nums) (target)) : Prop :=
  let i := result.fst;
  let j := result.snd;
  (i < j) ∧
  (i < nums.length) ∧ (j < nums.length) ∧
  (nums[i]!) + (nums[j]!) = target

lemma findComplement_some {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} {j : Nat} :
  findComplement xs target i x = some j → i < j ∧ j <= i + xs.length ∧ ∃ k, k < xs.length ∧ j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
  -- By definition of `findComplement`, if it returns `some j`, then `j` is the index of the complement of `x` in `xs`.
  intro h_findComplement
  obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ : ∃ k < xs.length, j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
    -- By definition of `findComplement`, if it returns `some j`, then there exists some `k` in `xs` where `x + k = target` and `j` is the index of that `k` in `xs`. This follows directly from the definition of `findComplement`.
    have h_findComplement_spec : ∀ {xs : List ℤ} {target : ℤ} {i : ℕ} {x : ℤ} {j : ℕ}, findComplement xs target i x = some j → List.length xs ≥ 1 ∧ ∃ k < List.length xs, j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
      intros xs target i x j h_findComplement; induction' xs with xs_hd xs_tl ih generalizing i x j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement ] ;
      · cases h_findComplement;
      · by_cases h : x + xs_hd = target <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ];
        obtain ⟨ k, hk₁, hk₂, hk₃ ⟩ := ih ( show findComplement.aux target ( i + 1 ) x xs_tl 0 = Option.some j from by
                                              convert h_findComplement using 1;
                                              -- By definition of `findComplement.aux`, we can prove this by induction on the list `xs_tl`.
                                              have h_aux_eq : ∀ {xs : List ℤ} {target : ℤ} {i : ℕ} {x : ℤ} {j : ℕ}, findComplement.aux target (i + 1) x xs j = findComplement.aux target i x xs (j + 1) := by

lemma findComplement_none {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} :
  findComplement xs target i x = none → ∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target := by
  intro h k hk_lt hk_eq
  have h_aux_none : ∀ (xs : List Int) (j : Nat), findComplement.aux target i x xs j = none → ∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target := by
    -- By induction on the list xs, we can show that if findComplement.aux returns none, then there's no index k in xs where x + xs[k]! equals the target.
    intros xs j hj_none k hk_lt hk_eq
    induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing j k;
    · contradiction;
    · rcases k with ( _ | k ) <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ];
      grind;
  -- Apply the lemma h_aux_none to the hypothesis h.
  apply h_aux_none xs 0 h k hk_lt hk_eq

lemma findComplement_eq_none {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} :
  (∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target) → findComplement xs target i x = none := by
  -- By definition of `findComplement`, if no element in `xs` satisfies `x + y = target`, then `findComplement xs target i x` returns `none`.
  intros h_no_complement
  induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing i x <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement ];
  · -- In the base case, when the list is empty, the function returns none.
    simp [findComplement.aux];
  · -- By definition of `findComplement.aux`, if no element in `y :: ys` satisfies `x + y = target`, then `findComplement.aux target i x (y :: ys) 0` returns `none`.
    have h_aux_none : ∀ {j : ℕ} {xs : List ℤ}, (∀ k < xs.length, x + xs[k]! ≠ target) → findComplement.aux target i x xs j = none := by
      intros j xs h_no_complement; induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ] ;
      -- Apply the induction hypothesis to the list `ys` and the parameter `j + 1`.
      have h_ind : findComplement.aux target i x ys (j + 1) = none := by

  (i < nums.length) ∧ (j < nums.length) ∧
  (nums[i]!) + (nums[j]!) = target

lemma findComplement_some {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} {j : Nat} :
  findComplement xs target i x = some j → i < j ∧ j <= i + xs.length ∧ ∃ k, k < xs.length ∧ j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
  -- By definition of `findComplement`, if it returns `some j`, then `j` is the index of the complement of `x` in `xs`.
  intro h_findComplement
  obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ : ∃ k < xs.length, j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
    -- By definition of `findComplement`, if it returns `some j`, then there exists some `k` in `xs` where `x + k = target` and `j` is the index of that `k` in `xs`. This follows directly from the definition of `findComplement`.
    have h_findComplement_spec : ∀ {xs : List ℤ} {target : ℤ} {i : ℕ} {x : ℤ} {j : ℕ}, findComplement xs target i x = some j → List.length xs ≥ 1 ∧ ∃ k < List.length xs, j = i + 1 + k ∧ x + xs[k]! = target := by
      intros xs target i x j h_findComplement; induction' xs with xs_hd xs_tl ih generalizing i x j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement ] ;
      · cases h_findComplement;
      · by_cases h : x + xs_hd = target <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ];
        obtain ⟨ k, hk₁, hk₂, hk₃ ⟩ := ih ( show findComplement.aux target ( i + 1 ) x xs_tl 0 = Option.some j from by
                                              convert h_findComplement using 1;
                                              -- By definition of `findComplement.aux`, we can prove this by induction on the list `xs_tl`.
                                              have h_aux_eq : ∀ {xs : List ℤ} {target : ℤ} {i : ℕ} {x : ℤ} {j : ℕ}, findComplement.aux target (i + 1) x xs j = findComplement.aux target i x xs (j + 1) := by
                                                intros xs target i x j; induction' xs with xs_hd xs_tl ih generalizing i x j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ] ;
                                                ring;
                                              exact h_aux_eq );
        exact ⟨ hk₁ + 1, by linarith, by linarith, by simpa [ List.getElem?_cons ] using hk₃.2 ⟩;
    exact h_findComplement_spec h_findComplement |>.2;
  exact ⟨ by linarith, by linarith, k, hk.1, hk.2.1, hk.2.2 ⟩

lemma findComplement_none {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} :
  findComplement xs target i x = none → ∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target := by
  intro h k hk_lt hk_eq
  have h_aux_none : ∀ (xs : List Int) (j : Nat), findComplement.aux target i x xs j = none → ∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target := by
    -- By induction on the list xs, we can show that if findComplement.aux returns none, then there's no index k in xs where x + xs[k]! equals the target.
    intros xs j hj_none k hk_lt hk_eq
    induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing j k;
    · contradiction;
    · rcases k with ( _ | k ) <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ];
      grind;
  -- Apply the lemma h_aux_none to the hypothesis h.
  apply h_aux_none xs 0 h k hk_lt hk_eq

lemma findComplement_eq_none {xs : List Int} {target : Int} {i : Nat} {x : Int} :
  (∀ k, k < xs.length → x + xs[k]! ≠ target) → findComplement xs target i x = none := by
  -- By definition of `findComplement`, if no element in `xs` satisfies `x + y = target`, then `findComplement xs target i x` returns `none`.
  intros h_no_complement
  induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing i x <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement ];
  · -- In the base case, when the list is empty, the function returns none.
    simp [findComplement.aux];
  · -- By definition of `findComplement.aux`, if no element in `y :: ys` satisfies `x + y = target`, then `findComplement.aux target i x (y :: ys) 0` returns `none`.
    have h_aux_none : ∀ {j : ℕ} {xs : List ℤ}, (∀ k < xs.length, x + xs[k]! ≠ target) → findComplement.aux target i x xs j = none := by
      intros j xs h_no_complement; induction' xs with y ys ih generalizing j <;> simp_all +decide [ findComplement.aux ] ;
      -- Apply the induction hypothesis to the list `ys` and the parameter `j + 1`.
      have h_ind : findComplement.aux target i x ys (j + 1) = none := by
        exact ih fun k hk => h_no_complement ( k + 1 ) ( by linarith );
      exact if_neg ( h_no_complement 0 ( Nat.zero_lt_succ _ ) ) |> fun h => h.trans h_ind;
    grind

lemma twoSumAux_prop (nums : List Int) (target : Int) (i : Nat) :
  let (u, v) := twoSumAux nums target i
  u < v → i ≤ u ∧ v < i + nums.length ∧ nums[u - i]! + nums[v - i]! = target := by
  induction' nums with x xs ih generalizing i <;> simp_all +decide [ List.range_succ_eq_map ];
  · exact fun h => False.elim <| h.not_le <| by rfl;
  · by_cases h : findComplement xs target i x = none <;> simp_all +decide [ twoSumAux ];
    · grind +ring;
    · -- Since `findComplement` returns a `j`, we know that `x + xs[j - i]! = target`.
      obtain ⟨j, hj⟩ : ∃ j, findComplement xs target i x = some j := by

The solution correctly implements the logic described in the problem statement, calculating the sum of squared distinct counts for all subarrays. The proof verifies that the implementation matches the postcondition specification.

import Mathlib

set_option linter.mathlibStandardSet false

open scoped BigOperators
open scoped Real
open scoped Nat
open scoped Classical
open scoped Pointwise

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option maxRecDepth 4000
set_option synthInstance.maxHeartbeats 20000
set_option synthInstance.maxSize 128

set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

open Std

@[reducible]
def solution_precond (nums : List Nat) : Prop :=
  1 ≤ nums.length ∧ nums.length ≤ 100 ∧ nums.all (fun x => 1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ 100)

def solution (nums : List Nat) : Nat :=
  let n := nums.length
  let subarray := fun (i j : Nat) => (nums.drop i).take (j - i + 1)
  let distinctCount := fun (l : List Nat) =>
    let hashSet := l.foldl (fun (s : HashSet Nat) a => s.insert a) HashSet.empty
    hashSet.size
  List.range n |>.foldl (fun acc i =>
    acc +
      (List.range (n - i) |>.foldl (fun acc' d =>
        let subarr := subarray i (i + d)
        let cnt := distinctCount subarr
        acc' + cnt * cnt
      ) 0)
  ) 0

@[reducible]
def solution_postcond (nums : List Nat) (result: Nat) : Prop :=
  let n := nums.length;

  let getSubarray_local := fun (i j : Nat) =>
    (nums.drop i).take (j - i + 1);

  let distinctCount_local := fun (l : List Nat) =>
    let foldFn := fun (seen : List Nat) (x : Nat) =>
      if seen.elem x then seen else x :: seen;
    let distinctElems := l.foldl foldFn [];
    distinctElems.length;

  let square_local := fun (n : Nat) => n * n;

  (1 <= n ∧ n <= 100 ∧ nums.all (fun x => 1 <= x ∧ x <= 100)) ->
  (
    result >= 0
    ∧
    let expectedSum : Nat :=
      List.range n |>.foldl (fun (outerSum : Nat) (i : Nat) =>
        let innerSum : Nat :=
          List.range (n - i) |>.foldl (fun (currentInnerSum : Nat) (d : Nat) =>
            let j := i + d;
            let subarr := getSubarray_local i j;
            let count := distinctCount_local subarr;
            currentInnerSum + square_local count
          ) 0
        outerSum + innerSum
      ) 0;

    result = expectedSum
  )

theorem solution_spec_satisfied (nums: List Nat) :
    solution_postcond (nums) (solution (nums)) := by
  -- By definition of `solution`, we know that it satisfies the postcondition.
  simp [solution_postcond, solution];
  -- By definition of `distinctCount`, we know that it correctly computes the number of distinct elements in a list.
  have h_distinctCount : ∀ (l : List ℕ), (List.foldl (fun (s : Std.HashSet ℕ) (a : ℕ) => s.insert a) Std.HashSet.empty l).size = (List.foldl (fun (seen : List ℕ) (x : ℕ) => if x ∈ seen then seen else x :: seen) [] l).length := by
    intro l;
    induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih;
    · rfl;
    · by_cases h : ih ∈ List.foldl ( fun seen x => if x ∈ seen then seen else x :: seen ) [ ] l <;> simp_all +decide [ Std.HashSet.size_insert ];
      · -- By definition of `foldl`, if `ih` is in the list after folding, then it must have been inserted into the set during the folding process.
        have h_insert : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (ih : ℕ), ih ∈ List.foldl (fun (seen : List ℕ) (x : ℕ) => if x ∈ seen then seen else x :: seen) [] l → ih ∈ List.foldl (fun (s : Std.HashSet ℕ) (a : ℕ) => s.insert a) Std.HashSet.empty l := by

/-
This module defines a function `smallestMissing` that finds the smallest missing natural number in a sorted list of natural numbers.
It also defines the precondition `smallestMissing_precond` (the list is sorted) and the postcondition `smallestMissing_postcond` (the result is not in the list, and all smaller natural numbers are in the list).
Finally, it proves `smallestMissing_spec_satisfied`, which states that `smallestMissing` satisfies the postcondition given the precondition.
-/

import Mathlib

set_option linter.mathlibStandardSet false

open scoped BigOperators
open scoped Real
open scoped Nat
open scoped Classical
open scoped Pointwise

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option maxRecDepth 4000
set_option synthInstance.maxHeartbeats 20000
set_option synthInstance.maxSize 128

set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

@[reducible]
def smallestMissing_precond (l : List Nat) : Prop :=
  List.Pairwise (· < ·) l

def smallestMissing (l : List Nat) (h_precond : smallestMissing_precond (l)) : Nat :=
  let sortedList := l
  let rec search (lst : List Nat) (n : Nat) : Nat :=
    match lst with
    | [] => n
    | x :: xs =>
      let isEqual := x = n
      let isGreater := x > n
      let nextCand := n + 1
      if isEqual then
        search xs nextCand
      else if isGreater then
        n
      else
        search xs n
  let result := search sortedList 0
  result

@[reducible]
def smallestMissing_postcond (l : List Nat) (result: Nat) (h_precond : smallestMissing_precond (l)) : Prop :=
  result ∉ l ∧ ∀ candidate : Nat, candidate < result → candidate ∈ l

theorem smallestMissing_spec_satisfied (l: List Nat) (h_precond : smallestMissing_precond (l)) :
    smallestMissing_postcond (l) (smallestMissing (l) h_precond) h_precond := by
  -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `l`.
  have h_search : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond l → smallestMissing.search l n ∉ l ∧ ∀ candidate < smallestMissing.search l n, n ≤ candidate → candidate ∈ l := by
    intros l n h_precond
    induction' l with hd tl ih generalizing n;
    · -- In the base case, when the list is empty, the smallest missing natural number is 0.
      simp [smallestMissing.search];
    · -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `l` and for all smaller candidates, they are in `l`.
      simp [smallestMissing.search];
      split_ifs <;> simp_all +decide [ smallestMissing_precond ];
      · constructor;
        · -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `tl`.
          have h_search_tl : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond l → smallestMissing.search l n ≥ n := by
            intros l n h_precond; induction' l with hd tl ih generalizing n; simp [smallestMissing.search];
            simp [smallestMissing.search];
            split_ifs <;> simp_all +decide [ smallestMissing_precond ];
            -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `tl` and for all smaller candidates, they are in `tl`.
            have h_search_tl : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond l → smallestMissing.search l n ≥ n := by

          linarith [ h_search_tl tl ( n + 1 ) h_precond.2 ];

      · exact ⟨ Ne.symm ‹_›, fun h => by linarith [ h_precond.1 _ h ] ⟩;
      · -- Since $hd < n$ and $hd \leq n$, we have $hd < n$.
        have h_hd_lt_n : hd < n := by

  have h_search : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond l → smallestMissing.search l n ∉ l ∧ ∀ candidate < smallestMissing.search l n, n ≤ candidate → candidate ∈ l := by
    intros l n h_precond
    induction' l with hd tl ih generalizing n;
    · -- In the base case, when the list is empty, the smallest missing natural number is 0.
      simp [smallestMissing.search];
    · -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `l` and for all smaller candidates, they are in `l`.
      simp [smallestMissing.search];
      split_ifs <;> simp_all +decide [ smallestMissing_precond ];
      · constructor;
        · -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `tl`.
          have h_search_tl : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond l → smallestMissing.search l n ≥ n := by
            intros l n h_precond; induction' l with hd tl ih generalizing n; simp [smallestMissing.search];
            simp [smallestMissing.search];
            split_ifs <;> simp_all +decide [ smallestMissing_precond ];
            -- By definition of `smallestMissing.search`, we know that it returns the smallest natural number not in `tl` and for all smaller candidates, they are in `tl`.
            have h_search_tl : ∀ (l : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond l → smallestMissing.search l n ≥ n := by
              intros l n h_precond; induction' l with hd tl ih generalizing n; simp [smallestMissing.search];
              simp [smallestMissing.search];
              grind;
            exact le_trans ( by linarith ) ( h_search_tl _ _ h_precond.2 );
          linarith [ h_search_tl tl ( n + 1 ) h_precond.2 ];
        · grind;
      · exact ⟨ Ne.symm ‹_›, fun h => by linarith [ h_precond.1 _ h ] ⟩;
      · -- Since $hd < n$ and $hd \leq n$, we have $hd < n$.
        have h_hd_lt_n : hd < n := by
          exact lt_of_le_of_ne ‹_› ‹_›;
        -- Since $hd < n$ and $hd \leq n$, we have $hd < n$. Therefore, $smallestMissing.search tl n$ cannot be equal to $hd$.
        have h_neq : smallestMissing.search tl n ≠ hd := by
          have h_neq : ∀ (tl : List ℕ) (n : ℕ), smallestMissing_precond tl → n ≤ smallestMissing.search tl n := by

/-
We define `shortestBeautifulSubstring` which finds the shortest substring of a binary string `s` containing exactly `k` ones. If there are multiple such substrings of the same minimal length, it returns the lexicographically smallest one. We prove that this function satisfies the postcondition `shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond`, meaning it returns a valid substring with the required properties, or the empty string if no such substring exists.
-/

import Mathlib

set_option linter.mathlibStandardSet false

open scoped BigOperators
open scoped Real
open scoped Nat
open scoped Classical
open scoped Pointwise

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option maxRecDepth 4000
set_option synthInstance.maxHeartbeats 20000
set_option synthInstance.maxSize 128

set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

def countOnes (lst : List Char) : Nat :=
  lst.foldl (fun acc c => if c = '1' then acc + 1 else acc) 0

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s : String) (k : Nat) : Prop :=
  s.toList.all (fun c => c = '0' ∨ c = '1')

def listToString (lst : List Char) : String :=
  String.mk lst

def isLexSmaller (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  listToString a < listToString b

def allSubstrings (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  let n := s.length
  (List.range n).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (n - i)).map (fun j =>
      s.drop i |>.take (j + 1)))

def shortestBeautifulSubstring (s : String) (k : Nat) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : String :=
  let chars := s.data
  let candidates := allSubstrings chars |>.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)

  let compare (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
    a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

  let best := candidates.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if compare cur best then some cur else some best) none
  match best with
  | some b => listToString b
  | none => ""

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond (s : String) (k : Nat) (result: String) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : Prop :=
  let chars := s.data
  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let isBeautiful := fun sub => countOnes sub = k
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => isBeautiful sub)
  let targets := beautiful.map (·.asString) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  (result = "" ∧ targets = []) ∨
  (result ∈ targets ∧
   ∀ r ∈ targets, r.length ≥ result.length ∨ (r.length = result.length ∧ result ≤ r))

def compareCandidates (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

def updateCandidate (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char) : Option (List Char) :=
  match acc with
  | none => some cur
  | some best => if compareCandidates cur best then some cur else some best

theorem shortestBeautifulSubstring_foldl_eq (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if cur.length < best.length ∨ (cur.length = best.length ∧ isLexSmaller cur best) then some cur else some best) none
  = l.foldl updateCandidate none := by

  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → best ∈ l := by

    have h_foldl : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate acc l = some best → acc = some best ∨ best ∈ l := by

      induction' l with l ih generalizing acc best <;> simp [updateCandidate] at *;

            specialize h_empty l h'; aesop;
          · split_ifs at h_foldl <;> simp_all +decide [ compareCandidates ];
            -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude the proof.
            apply And.intro;
            · grind;
            · -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude that if x.length equals best.length, then isLexSmaller x best is false.
              intros h_eq
              have h_lex : isLexSmaller x best = Bool.false := by

set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

def countOnes (lst : List Char) : Nat :=
  lst.foldl (fun acc c => if c = '1' then acc + 1 else acc) 0

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s : String) (k : Nat) : Prop :=
  s.toList.all (fun c => c = '0' ∨ c = '1')

def listToString (lst : List Char) : String :=
  String.mk lst

def isLexSmaller (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  listToString a < listToString b

def allSubstrings (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  let n := s.length
  (List.range n).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (n - i)).map (fun j =>
      s.drop i |>.take (j + 1)))

def shortestBeautifulSubstring (s : String) (k : Nat) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : String :=
  let chars := s.data
  let candidates := allSubstrings chars |>.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)

  let compare (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
    a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

  let best := candidates.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if compare cur best then some cur else some best) none
  match best with
  | some b => listToString b
  | none => ""

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond (s : String) (k : Nat) (result: String) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : Prop :=
  let chars := s.data
  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let isBeautiful := fun sub => countOnes sub = k
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => isBeautiful sub)
  let targets := beautiful.map (·.asString) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  (result = "" ∧ targets = []) ∨
  (result ∈ targets ∧
   ∀ r ∈ targets, r.length ≥ result.length ∨ (r.length = result.length ∧ result ≤ r))

def compareCandidates (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

def updateCandidate (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char) : Option (List Char) :=
  match acc with
  | none => some cur
  | some best => if compareCandidates cur best then some cur else some best

theorem shortestBeautifulSubstring_foldl_eq (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if cur.length < best.length ∨ (cur.length = best.length ∧ isLexSmaller cur best) then some cur else some best) none
  = l.foldl updateCandidate none := by
    congr;
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression.
    funext acc cur; exact (by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best`.
    cases acc <;> simp [updateCandidate];
    -- By definition of `compareCandidates`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best` and simplify the if statement.
    simp [compareCandidates])

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_mem (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → best ∈ l := by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the foldl results in `some best`, then `best` must have been in the list `ih` because each step of the foldl adds the best candidate from the current list.
    have h_foldl : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate acc l = some best → acc = some best ∨ best ∈ l := by
      -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l with some x is some best, then x must be some best or best is in l.
      intro acc l h_foldl
      induction' l with l ih generalizing acc best <;> simp [updateCandidate] at *;
      · exact h_foldl;
      · cases acc <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
        · grind +ring;
        · grind;
    grind

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_optimal (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = false := by
    intros h x hx;
    have h_foldl : ∀ (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char), List.foldl updateCandidate none l = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = Bool.false := by
      -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is some best, then for any x in l, compareCandidates x best is false.
      intros l best h_foldl x hx
      induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best;
      · contradiction;
      · by_cases h : x ∈ l <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl_append ];
        · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is none, then l must be empty.
            have h_empty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l = Option.none → l = [] := by
              intros l hl; induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best; aesop;
              cases h : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
              grind;
            specialize h_empty l h'; aesop;
          · split_ifs at h_foldl <;> simp_all +decide [ compareCandidates ];
            -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude the proof.
            apply And.intro;
            · grind;
            · -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude that if x.length equals best.length, then isLexSmaller x best is false.
              intros h_eq
              have h_lex : isLexSmaller x best = Bool.false := by
                cases ‹best.length < _ ∨ _› <;> simp_all +decide [ isLexSmaller ];
                · grind;
                · exact le_trans ( by tauto ) ( by tauto )
              exact h_lex;
        · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l.
          have h_update : best = ih ∨ best = (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l).get! := by
            -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. This follows directly from the definition of updateCandidate.
            have h_update : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char), updateCandidate acc cur = some best → best = cur ∨ best = acc.get! := by

/-
We define `shortestBeautifulSubstring` which finds the shortest substring of a binary string `s` containing exactly `k` ones. If there are multiple such substrings of the same minimal length, it returns the lexicographically smallest one. We prove that this function satisfies the postcondition `shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond`, meaning it returns a valid substring with the required properties, or the empty string if no such substring exists.
-/

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_optimal (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = false := by

    have h_foldl : ∀ (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char), List.foldl updateCandidate none l = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = Bool.false := by

      intros l best h_foldl x hx
      induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best;
      · contradiction;
      · by_cases h : x ∈ l <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl_append ];
        · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is none, then l must be empty.
            have h_empty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l = Option.none → l = [] := by
              intros l hl; induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best; aesop;
              cases h : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
              grind;
            specialize h_empty l h'; aesop;
          · split_ifs at h_foldl <;> simp_all +decide [ compareCandidates ];
            -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude the proof.
            apply And.intro;
            · grind;
            · -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude that if x.length equals best.length, then isLexSmaller x best is false.
              intros h_eq
              have h_lex : isLexSmaller x best = Bool.false := by
                cases ‹best.length < _ ∨ _› <;> simp_all +decide [ isLexSmaller ];
                · grind;
                · exact le_trans ( by tauto ) ( by tauto )
              exact h_lex;
        · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l.
          have h_update : best = ih ∨ best = (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l).get! := by
            -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. This follows directly from the definition of updateCandidate.
            have h_update : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char), updateCandidate acc cur = some best → best = cur ∨ best = acc.get! := by
              unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
            exact h_update _ _ h_foldl;
          -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false.
          cases h_update <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · unfold compareCandidates; simp +decide ;
            unfold isLexSmaller; simp +decide ;
          · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
            · native_decide +revert;
            · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false because if ih is equal to best, then compareCandidates ih best is false.
              by_contra h_contra;
              have := foldl_updateCandidate_mem l _ h'; aesop;
    exact h_foldl l best h x hx

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_none (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = none ↔ l = [] := by
    -- We'll use induction on the list `l`.
    induction' l with x l ih ih;
    · exact?;
    · -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the list is non-empty, the foldl cannot be none.
      have h_nonempty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), l ≠ [] → List.foldl updateCandidate none l ≠ none := by

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_none (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = none ↔ l = [] := by
    -- We'll use induction on the list `l`.
    induction' l with x l ih ih;
    · exact?;
    · -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the list is non-empty, the foldl cannot be none.
      have h_nonempty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), l ≠ [] → List.foldl updateCandidate none l ≠ none := by
        -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the list is non-empty, the foldl will process the first element and set the accumulator to that element, then process the rest of the list.
        intro l hl_nonempty
        induction' l with x l ih;
        · contradiction;
        · induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with x l ih <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl ];
          · exact?;
          · unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
      specialize h_nonempty ( x :: l ) ; aesop

def allSubstrings_spec (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  (List.range s.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (s.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      s.drop i |>.take len))

theorem allSubstrings_eq_filter_spec (s : List Char) :
  allSubstrings s = (allSubstrings_spec s).filter (fun x => x ≠ []) := by
    -- By definition of `allSubstrings`, we know that it is equal to `allSubstrings_spec` filtered by non-empty sublists because `allSubstrings` includes only non-empty sublists.
    simp [allSubstrings, allSubstrings_spec];
    -- The two lists are equal because the flatMap of the first list already excludes the empty lists that are present in the second list.
    have h_eq : ∀ i ∈ List.range s.length, List.map (fun j => List.take (j + 1) (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i)) = List.filter (fun x => !Decidable.decide (x = [])) (List.map (fun len => List.take len (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i + 1))) := by

/-
We define `shortestBeautifulSubstring` which finds the shortest substring of a binary string `s` containing exactly `k` ones. If there are multiple such substrings of the same minimal length, it returns the lexicographically smallest one. We prove that this function satisfies the postcondition `shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond`, meaning it returns a valid substring with the required properties, or the empty string if no such substring exists.
-/

import Mathlib

set_option linter.mathlibStandardSet false

open scoped BigOperators
open scoped Real
open scoped Nat
open scoped Classical
open scoped Pointwise

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option maxRecDepth 4000
set_option synthInstance.maxHeartbeats 20000
set_option synthInstance.maxSize 128

set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

def countOnes (lst : List Char) : Nat :=
  lst.foldl (fun acc c => if c = '1' then acc + 1 else acc) 0

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s : String) (k : Nat) : Prop :=
  s.toList.all (fun c => c = '0' ∨ c = '1')

def listToString (lst : List Char) : String :=
  String.mk lst

def isLexSmaller (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  listToString a < listToString b

def allSubstrings (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  let n := s.length
  (List.range n).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (n - i)).map (fun j =>
      s.drop i |>.take (j + 1)))

def shortestBeautifulSubstring (s : String) (k : Nat) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : String :=
  let chars := s.data
  let candidates := allSubstrings chars |>.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)

  let compare (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
    a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

  let best := candidates.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if compare cur best then some cur else some best) none
  match best with
  | some b => listToString b
  | none => ""

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond (s : String) (k : Nat) (result: String) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : Prop :=
  let chars := s.data
  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let isBeautiful := fun sub => countOnes sub = k
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => isBeautiful sub)
  let targets := beautiful.map (·.asString) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  (result = "" ∧ targets = []) ∨
  (result ∈ targets ∧
   ∀ r ∈ targets, r.length ≥ result.length ∨ (r.length = result.length ∧ result ≤ r))

def compareCandidates (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

def updateCandidate (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char) : Option (List Char) :=
  match acc with
  | none => some cur
  | some best => if compareCandidates cur best then some cur else some best

theorem shortestBeautifulSubstring_foldl_eq (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if cur.length < best.length ∨ (cur.length = best.length ∧ isLexSmaller cur best) then some cur else some best) none
  = l.foldl updateCandidate none := by
    congr;
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression.
    funext acc cur; exact (by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best`.
    cases acc <;> simp [updateCandidate];
    -- By definition of `compareCandidates`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best` and simplify the if statement.
    simp [compareCandidates])

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_mem (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → best ∈ l := by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the foldl results in `some best`, then `best` must have been in the list `ih` because each step of the foldl adds the best candidate from the current list.
    have h_foldl : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate acc l = some best → acc = some best ∨ best ∈ l := by
      -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l with some x is some best, then x must be some best or best is in l.
      intro acc l h_foldl
      induction' l with l ih generalizing acc best <;> simp [updateCandidate] at *;
      · exact h_foldl;
      · cases acc <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
        · grind +ring;
        · grind;
    grind

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_optimal (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = false := by
    intros h x hx;
    have h_foldl : ∀ (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char), List.foldl updateCandidate none l = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = Bool.false := by
      -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is some best, then for any x in l, compareCandidates x best is false.
      intros l best h_foldl x hx
      induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best;
      · contradiction;
      · by_cases h : x ∈ l <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl_append ];
        · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is none, then l must be empty.
            have h_empty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l = Option.none → l = [] := by
              intros l hl; induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best; aesop;
              cases h : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
              grind;
            specialize h_empty l h'; aesop;
          · split_ifs at h_foldl <;> simp_all +decide [ compareCandidates ];
            -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude the proof.
            apply And.intro;
            · grind;
            · -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude that if x.length equals best.length, then isLexSmaller x best is false.
              intros h_eq
              have h_lex : isLexSmaller x best = Bool.false := by
                cases ‹best.length < _ ∨ _› <;> simp_all +decide [ isLexSmaller ];
                · grind;
                · exact le_trans ( by tauto ) ( by tauto )
              exact h_lex;
        · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l.
          have h_update : best = ih ∨ best = (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l).get! := by
            -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. This follows directly from the definition of updateCandidate.
            have h_update : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char), updateCandidate acc cur = some best → best = cur ∨ best = acc.get! := by
              unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
            exact h_update _ _ h_foldl;
          -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false.
          cases h_update <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · unfold compareCandidates; simp +decide ;
            unfold isLexSmaller; simp +decide ;
          · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
            · native_decide +revert;
            · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false because if ih is equal to best, then compareCandidates ih best is false.
              by_contra h_contra;
              have := foldl_updateCandidate_mem l _ h'; aesop;
    exact h_foldl l best h x hx

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_none (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = none ↔ l = [] := by
    -- We'll use induction on the list `l`.
    induction' l with x l ih ih;
    · exact?;
    · -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the list is non-empty, the foldl cannot be none.
      have h_nonempty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), l ≠ [] → List.foldl updateCandidate none l ≠ none := by
        -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the list is non-empty, the foldl will process the first element and set the accumulator to that element, then process the rest of the list.
        intro l hl_nonempty
        induction' l with x l ih;
        · contradiction;
        · induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with x l ih <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl ];
          · exact?;
          · unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
      specialize h_nonempty ( x :: l ) ; aesop

def allSubstrings_spec (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  (List.range s.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (s.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      s.drop i |>.take len))

theorem allSubstrings_eq_filter_spec (s : List Char) :
  allSubstrings s = (allSubstrings_spec s).filter (fun x => x ≠ []) := by
    -- By definition of `allSubstrings`, we know that it is equal to `allSubstrings_spec` filtered by non-empty sublists because `allSubstrings` includes only non-empty sublists.
    simp [allSubstrings, allSubstrings_spec];
    -- The two lists are equal because the flatMap of the first list already excludes the empty lists that are present in the second list.
    have h_eq : ∀ i ∈ List.range s.length, List.map (fun j => List.take (j + 1) (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i)) = List.filter (fun x => !Decidable.decide (x = [])) (List.map (fun len => List.take len (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i + 1))) := by
      intro i hi; induction s.length - i <;> simp_all +decide [ List.range_succ_eq_map ] ;
      rw [ List.filter_eq_self.mpr ] ; aesop;
      simp_all +decide [ Function.comp ];
    -- By applying h_eq to each element in the flatMap, we can replace each map with the filtered map.
    have h_flatMap_eq : List.flatMap (fun i => List.map (fun j => List.take (j + 1) (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i))) (List.range s.length) = List.flatMap (fun i => List.filter (fun x => !Decidable.decide (x = [])) (List.map (fun len => List.take len (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i + 1)))) (List.range s.length) := by

theorem compareCandidates_false_implies_le (a b : List Char) :
  compareCandidates a b = false → b.length < a.length ∨ (b.length = a.length ∧ listToString b ≤ listToString a) := by
    -- By definition of compareCandidates, if compareCandidates a b is false, then either a.length > b.length or a.length = b.length and a is not less than b lexicographically. This follows directly from the definition of compareCandidates.
    simp [compareCandidates];
    -- We can split into cases based on whether `b.length` is less than or equal to `a.length`.
    by_cases h : b.length < a.length;
    · grind;
    · -- Since $b.length$ is not less than $a.length$, we have $b.length \geq a.length$. If $b.length = a.length$, then by the definition of compareCandidates, $a$ is not less than $b$ lexicographically.
      by_cases h_eq : b.length = a.length;
      · -- Since $a$ is not less than $b$ lexicographically, we have $b \leq a$.
        have h_lex : isLexSmaller a b = Bool.false → listToString b ≤ listToString a := by

import Mathlib

set_option linter.mathlibStandardSet false

open scoped BigOperators
open scoped Real
open scoped Nat
open scoped Classical
open scoped Pointwise

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option maxRecDepth 4000
set_option synthInstance.maxHeartbeats 20000
set_option synthInstance.maxSize 128

set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

noncomputable section

def countOnes (lst : List Char) : Nat :=
  lst.foldl (fun acc c => if c = '1' then acc + 1 else acc) 0

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s : String) (k : Nat) : Prop :=
  s.toList.all (fun c => c = '0' ∨ c = '1')

def listToString (lst : List Char) : String :=
  String.mk lst

def isLexSmaller (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  listToString a < listToString b

def allSubstrings (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  let n := s.length
  (List.range n).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (n - i)).map (fun j =>
      s.drop i |>.take (j + 1)))

def shortestBeautifulSubstring (s : String) (k : Nat) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : String :=
  let chars := s.data
  let candidates := allSubstrings chars |>.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)

  let compare (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
    a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

  let best := candidates.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if compare cur best then some cur else some best) none
  match best with
  | some b => listToString b
  | none => ""

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond (s : String) (k : Nat) (result: String) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : Prop :=
  let chars := s.data
  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let isBeautiful := fun sub => countOnes sub = k
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => isBeautiful sub)
  let targets := beautiful.map (·.asString) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  (result = "" ∧ targets = []) ∨
  (result ∈ targets ∧
   ∀ r ∈ targets, r.length ≥ result.length ∨ (r.length = result.length ∧ result ≤ r))

def compareCandidates (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

def updateCandidate (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char) : Option (List Char) :=
  match acc with
  | none => some cur
  | some best => if compareCandidates cur best then some cur else some best

theorem shortestBeautifulSubstring_foldl_eq (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if cur.length < best.length ∨ (cur.length = best.length ∧ isLexSmaller cur best) then some cur else some best) none
  = l.foldl updateCandidate none := by
    congr;
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression.
    funext acc cur; exact (by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best`.
    cases acc <;> simp [updateCandidate];
    -- By definition of `compareCandidates`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best` and simplify the if statement.
    simp [compareCandidates])

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_mem (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → best ∈ l := by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the foldl results in `some best`, then `best` must have been in the list `ih` because each step of the foldl adds the best candidate from the current list.
    have h_foldl : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate acc l = some best → acc = some best ∨ best ∈ l := by
      -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l with some x is some best, then x must be some best or best is in l.
      intro acc l h_foldl
      induction' l with l ih generalizing acc best <;> simp [updateCandidate] at *;
      · exact h_foldl;
      · cases acc <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
        · grind +ring;
        · grind;
    grind

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_optimal (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = false := by
    intros h x hx;
    have h_foldl : ∀ (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char), List.foldl updateCandidate none l = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = Bool.false := by
      -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is some best, then for any x in l, compareCandidates x best is false.
      intros l best h_foldl x hx
      induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best;
      · contradiction;
      · by_cases h : x ∈ l <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl_append ];
        · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is none, then l must be empty.
            have h_empty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l = Option.none → l = [] := by
              intros l hl; induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best; aesop;
              cases h : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
              grind;
            specialize h_empty l h'; aesop;
          · split_ifs at h_foldl <;> simp_all +decide [ compareCandidates ];
            -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude the proof.
            apply And.intro;
            · grind;
            · -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude that if x.length equals best.length, then isLexSmaller x best is false.
              intros h_eq
              have h_lex : isLexSmaller x best = Bool.false := by
                cases ‹best.length < _ ∨ _› <;> simp_all +decide [ isLexSmaller ];
                · grind;
                · exact le_trans ( by tauto ) ( by tauto )
              exact h_lex;
        · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l.
          have h_update : best = ih ∨ best = (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l).get! := by
            -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. This follows directly from the definition of updateCandidate.
            have h_update : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char), updateCandidate acc cur = some best → best = cur ∨ best = acc.get! := by
              unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
            exact h_update _ _ h_foldl;
          -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false.
          cases h_update <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · unfold compareCandidates; simp +decide ;
            unfold isLexSmaller; simp +decide ;
          · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
            · native_decide +revert;
            · -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false because if ih is equal to best, then compareCandidates ih best is false.
              by_contra h_contra;
              have := foldl_updateCandidate_mem l _ h'; aesop;
    exact h_foldl l best h x hx

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_none (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = none ↔ l = [] := by
    -- We'll use induction on the list `l`.
    induction' l with x l ih ih;
    · exact?;
    · -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the list is non-empty, the foldl cannot be none.
      have h_nonempty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), l ≠ [] → List.foldl updateCandidate none l ≠ none := by
        -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the list is non-empty, the foldl will process the first element and set the accumulator to that element, then process the rest of the list.
        intro l hl_nonempty
        induction' l with x l ih;
        · contradiction;
        · induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with x l ih <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl ];
          · exact?;
          · unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
      specialize h_nonempty ( x :: l ) ; aesop

def allSubstrings_spec (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  (List.range s.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (s.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      s.drop i |>.take len))

theorem allSubstrings_eq_filter_spec (s : List Char) :
  allSubstrings s = (allSubstrings_spec s).filter (fun x => x ≠ []) := by
    -- By definition of `allSubstrings`, we know that it is equal to `allSubstrings_spec` filtered by non-empty sublists because `allSubstrings` includes only non-empty sublists.
    simp [allSubstrings, allSubstrings_spec];
    -- The two lists are equal because the flatMap of the first list already excludes the empty lists that are present in the second list.
    have h_eq : ∀ i ∈ List.range s.length, List.map (fun j => List.take (j + 1) (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i)) = List.filter (fun x => !Decidable.decide (x = [])) (List.map (fun len => List.take len (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i + 1))) := by
      intro i hi; induction s.length - i <;> simp_all +decide [ List.range_succ_eq_map ] ;
      rw [ List.filter_eq_self.mpr ] ; aesop;
      simp_all +decide [ Function.comp ];
    -- By applying h_eq to each element in the flatMap, we can replace each map with the filtered map.
    have h_flatMap_eq : List.flatMap (fun i => List.map (fun j => List.take (j + 1) (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i))) (List.range s.length) = List.flatMap (fun i => List.filter (fun x => !Decidable.decide (x = [])) (List.map (fun len => List.take len (List.drop i s)) (List.range (s.length - i + 1)))) (List.range s.length) := by
      exact?;
    exact h_flatMap_eq.trans ( by induction ( List.range s.length ) <;> aesop )

theorem compareCandidates_false_implies_le (a b : List Char) :
  compareCandidates a b = false → b.length < a.length ∨ (b.length = a.length ∧ listToString b ≤ listToString a) := by
    -- By definition of compareCandidates, if compareCandidates a b is false, then either a.length > b.length or a.length = b.length and a is not less than b lexicographically. This follows directly from the definition of compareCandidates.
    simp [compareCandidates];
    -- We can split into cases based on whether `b.length` is less than or equal to `a.length`.
    by_cases h : b.length < a.length;
    · grind;
    · -- Since $b.length$ is not less than $a.length$, we have $b.length \geq a.length$. If $b.length = a.length$, then by the definition of compareCandidates, $a$ is not less than $b$ lexicographically.
      by_cases h_eq : b.length = a.length;
      · -- Since $a$ is not less than $b$ lexicographically, we have $b \leq a$.
        have h_lex : isLexSmaller a b = Bool.false → listToString b ≤ listToString a := by
          -- By definition of `isLexSmaller`, if `isLexSmaller a b` is false, then `a` is not less than `b`, which means `b` is less than or equal to `a`.
          simp [isLexSmaller];
        aesop;
      · exact fun h' => False.elim <| h_eq <| le_antisymm h' <| le_of_not_gt h

theorem targets_eq_candidates (s : String) (k : Nat) :
  let chars := s.data
  let candidates := allSubstrings chars |>.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)
  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)
  let targets := beautiful.map (String.mk) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  targets = candidates.map String.mk := by
    -- By definition of `allSubstrings`, we can rewrite the goal using `allSubstrings_spec`.
    have h_allSubstrings_eq : allSubstrings s.data = (allSubstrings_spec s.data).filter (fun x => x ≠ []) := by

/-
We define `shortestBeautifulSubstring` which finds the shortest substring of a binary string `s` containing exactly `k` ones. If there are multiple such substrings of the same minimal length, it returns the lexicographically smallest one. We prove that this function satisfies the postcondition `shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond`, meaning it returns a valid substring with the required properties, or the empty string if no such substring exists.
-/

  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)
  let targets := beautiful.map (String.mk) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  targets = candidates.map String.mk := by

    have h_allSubstrings_eq : allSubstrings s.data = (allSubstrings_spec s.data).filter (fun x => x ≠ []) := by
      exact?;
    -- By definition of `allSubstrings`, we can rewrite the goal using `allSubstrings_spec` and the fact that filtering for non-empty substrings does not change the list.
    simp [h_allSubstrings_eq, List.filter_filter];
    -- By definition of `allSubstrings_spec`, we can rewrite the goal using `allSubstrings_spec` and the fact that filtering for non-empty substrings does not change the list.
    simp [allSubstrings_spec];
    -- By definition of `List.filter`, we can rewrite the left-hand side to match the right-hand side.
    simp [List.filter_map];
    simp +decide [ String.ext_iff ];
    grind

/-
We define `shortestBeautifulSubstring` which finds the shortest substring of a binary string `s` containing exactly `k` ones. If there are multiple such substrings of the same minimal length, it returns the lexicographically smallest one. We prove that this function satisfies the postcondition `shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond`, meaning it returns a valid substring with the required properties, or the empty string if no such substring exists.
-/

import Mathlib

def countOnes (lst : List Char) : Nat :=
  lst.foldl (fun acc c => if c = '1' then acc + 1 else acc) 0

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s : String) (k : Nat) : Prop :=
  s.toList.all (fun c => c = '0' ∨ c = '1')

def listToString (lst : List Char) : String :=
  String.mk lst

def isLexSmaller (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  listToString a < listToString b

def allSubstrings (s : List Char) : List (List Char) :=
  let n := s.length
  (List.range n).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (n - i)).map (fun j =>
      s.drop i |>.take (j + 1)))

def shortestBeautifulSubstring (s : String) (k : Nat) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : String :=
  let chars := s.data
  let candidates := allSubstrings chars |>.filter (fun sub => countOnes sub = k)

  let compare (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
    a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

  let best := candidates.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if compare cur best then some cur else some best) none
  match best with
  | some b => listToString b
  | none => ""

@[reducible]
def shortestBeautifulSubstring_postcond (s : String) (k : Nat) (result: String) (h_precond : shortestBeautifulSubstring_precond (s) (k)) : Prop :=
  let chars := s.data
  let substrings := (List.range chars.length).flatMap (fun i =>
    (List.range (chars.length - i + 1)).map (fun len =>
      chars.drop i |>.take len))
  let isBeautiful := fun sub => countOnes sub = k
  let beautiful := substrings.filter (fun sub => isBeautiful sub)
  let targets := beautiful.map (·.asString) |>.filter (fun s => s ≠ "")
  (result = "" ∧ targets = []) ∨
  (result ∈ targets ∧
   ∀ r ∈ targets, r.length ≥ result.length ∨ (r.length = result.length ∧ result ≤ r))

def compareCandidates (a b : List Char) : Bool :=
  a.length < b.length ∨ (a.length = b.length ∧ isLexSmaller a b)

def updateCandidate (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char) : Option (List Char) :=
  match acc with
  | none => some cur
  | some best => if compareCandidates cur best then some cur else some best

theorem shortestBeautifulSubstring_foldl_eq (l : List (List Char)) :
  l.foldl (fun acc cur =>
    match acc with
    | none => some cur
    | some best => if cur.length < best.length ∨ (cur.length = best.length ∧ isLexSmaller cur best) then some cur else some best) none
  = l.foldl updateCandidate none := by
    congr;

    funext acc cur; exact (by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best`.
    cases acc <;> simp [updateCandidate];
    -- By definition of `compareCandidates`, we can rewrite the goal using the match expression. We'll split into cases based on whether `acc` is `none` or `some best` and simplify the if statement.
    simp [compareCandidates])

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_mem (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → best ∈ l := by
    -- By definition of `updateCandidate`, if the foldl results in `some best`, then `best` must have been in the list `ih` because each step of the foldl adds the best candidate from the current list.
    have h_foldl : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate acc l = some best → acc = some best ∨ best ∈ l := by
      -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l with some x is some best, then x must be some best or best is in l.
      intro acc l h_foldl
      induction' l with l ih generalizing acc best <;> simp [updateCandidate] at *;
      · exact h_foldl;
      · cases acc <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
        · grind +ring;
        · grind;
    grind

theorem foldl_updateCandidate_optimal (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char) :
  l.foldl updateCandidate none = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = false := by
    intros h x hx;
    have h_foldl : ∀ (l : List (List Char)) (best : List Char), List.foldl updateCandidate none l = some best → ∀ x ∈ l, compareCandidates x best = Bool.false := by
      -- By induction on the list, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is some best, then for any x in l, compareCandidates x best is false.
      intros l best h_foldl x hx
      induction' l using List.reverseRecOn with l ih generalizing best;
      · contradiction;
      · by_cases h : x ∈ l <;> simp_all +decide [ List.foldl_append ];
        · cases h' : List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];
          · -- By induction on the list l, we can show that if the foldl of updateCandidate on l is none, then l must be empty.
            have h_empty : ∀ (l : List (List Char)), List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l = Option.none → l = [] := by

            apply And.intro;
            · grind;
            · -- By combining the results from the induction hypothesis and the current case, we can conclude that if x.length equals best.length, then isLexSmaller x best is false.
              intros h_eq
              have h_lex : isLexSmaller x best = Bool.false := by

                · grind;
                · exact le_trans ( by tauto ) ( by tauto )
              exact h_lex;

          have h_update : best = ih ∨ best = (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l).get! := by
            -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. This follows directly from the definition of updateCandidate.
            have h_update : ∀ (acc : Option (List Char)) (cur : List Char), updateCandidate acc cur = some best → best = cur ∨ best = acc.get! := by
              unfold updateCandidate; aesop;
            exact h_update _ _ h_foldl;
          -- By definition of updateCandidate, if updateCandidate (List.foldl updateCandidate Option.none l) ih = some best, then best is either ih or the result of folding l. In both cases, compareCandidates ih best is false.
          cases h_update <;> simp_all +decide [ updateCandidate ];

              by_contra h_contra;
